Keep the box at the same position, scale and angle it occupies in the FIRST reference. Change only three things.

FIRST - THE HANDS. A woman's hands, about 42, with deep brown skin and elegant slender fingers, slender fingers, narrow wrists, smooth bare skin, short clean natural nails with no polish, no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch - the cuffs of a soft lavender cable-knit wool sweater pushed back at both wrists. Same position on the box, same grip, same arms entering the frame the same way, same scale.

SECOND - THE TABLE. The surface under the box is now a honey-oak parquet dining table with an orderly herringbone grain pattern, matte oiled timber and a clean square edge. Same height, same angle, same amount of the surface visible as in the FIRST reference.

THIRD - THE BACKGROUND. Behind and above the table, a cosy Christmas dining room with warm peach plaster walls, dark walnut wall panelling on the lower third, a pale stone fireplace, plum-coloured knitted stockings, a framed botanical print with no lettering and a fir tree dressed in red glass ornaments and gold ribbons. Every background detail stays in focus; the box remains dominant through its scale and placement.

Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few white berries, two or three small antique gold and pearl-white baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own carton.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and carton artwork only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. All parts of this photograph, including the background, are in crisp natural iPhone deep focus, no portrait mode, no bokeh, no blur.

```text

AUDIO RULE - HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: this clip has NO MUSIC OF ANY KIND. No background song, no soundtrack, no score, no melody, no instruments, no singing, no humming, no jingle, no Christmas song, no festive music bed, no ambient musical pad, no strings, no piano, no bells used as music. The audio track contains ONLY real natural sound recorded in that room. No video reference is attached. If any music appears anywhere in the 4 seconds the clip is wrong.

THE LEFT HAND stays braced against the lower left side of THE BOX from first frame to last, fingers resting below the main printed artwork. The left hand never lifts, follows the projector, gestures, reaches into the box or leaves the carton. It steadies the box on the table while the right hand and product rise. Exactly two hands with separate jobs: right hand carries the product, left hand anchors the carton.

EXIT RULE - THIRD MOST IMPORTANT: the projector does NOT stay in frame at the end. THE RIGHT HAND lifts it out and keeps going straight up until the projector and right hand leave the top of frame. At 4.0s the open carton remains on the table, held steady by THE LEFT HAND. The right hand never pauses to present the product.

SPEED RULE - FOURTH MOST IMPORTANT: the hands work at a brisk, confident, real-time pace. This is a normal person opening a box at normal speed, not a careful slow-motion demonstration. The lift is QUICK and decisive. Do not slow anything down.

TASK: image-to-video. The start image is frame 0.0s. Hold its table, box artwork, props and lighting exactly.

[1] SUMMARY
First-person POV: two hands open a tall dark-green Christmas carton on a honey-oak parquet dining table with an orderly herringbone grain pattern, matte oiled timber and a clean square edge, lift the white projector briskly straight up out of it and carry it out of the top of frame. Locked POV looking down. 4 seconds, one take.

[2] GLOBAL STYLE
Real handheld phone POV, warm domestic Christmas light, crisp and sharp, correctly exposed. Very slight handheld drift. Real-time speed throughout. No cinematic grade, no grain, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

[3] ASSET ROLES
The start image AND @Image 1 are the same approved image, attached as start_image and image reference. They alone control frame 0.0s, the exact printed box, hands, table, room, props and light. Begin on that exact view and animate it. No reference video, no alternate carton, no plain-box substitute.

[4] OBJECTS
THE BOX - a dark-green tuck-top carton standing upright, front panel facing camera, a dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, the cream serif words CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed white dome-topped projector on a white four-legged splayed stand, a gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. About 15 x 15 x 24 cm. Plain interior, no insert, no foam tray.
THE PROJECTOR - inside THE BOX: a matte-white dome-shaped body on a separate white four-legged splayed stand. Set into the top of the dome is a flat circular BLACK perforated mesh disc. The top face is BLACK and stays black for every frame. No front lens, no camera head, no swivel.
THE HANDS - exactly two hands, a woman's, with the cuff of a soft lavender cable-knit wool sweater pushed back at each wrist and no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch.

[5] LOCATION - a honey-oak parquet dining table with an orderly herringbone grain pattern, matte oiled timber and a clean square edge fills the frame. The lit cream candle, fir sprigs, white berries, gold and pearl-white baubles and warm string lights stay exactly where they are in the start image. Behind them, sharply in focus, a cosy Christmas dining room with warm peach plaster walls, dark walnut wall panelling on the lower third, a pale stone fireplace, plum-coloured knitted stockings, a framed botanical print with no lettering and a fir tree dressed in red glass ornaments and gold ribbons. The same table, carton, decorations and background remain visible through the final frame.

[6] FIRST FRAME - exactly the supplied image: THE BOX closed and upright with its full printed front facing the camera; the right hand on the lid and the left hand bracing the lower side in their exact shown positions.

[7] TIMELINE - brisk real-time pacing, every beat lands on time
0.0-0.7s - camera locked. THE LEFT HAND remains braced on the lower left side of THE BOX. THE RIGHT HAND slides its fingers under the front top flap and lifts it sharply; the tuck tongue pops out of the front panel.
0.7-1.4s - the lid flaps fold upward and backward away from camera on the rear fold in one quick motion, staying attached to THE BOX; the dark interior comes into view.
1.4-2.1s - THE RIGHT HAND reaches into THE BOX and grips the upper side of THE PROJECTOR firmly with thumb and fingers, leaving the BLACK perforated mesh disc visible. THE LEFT HAND remains on the outside of THE BOX.
2.1-3.0s - THE RIGHT HAND lifts THE PROJECTOR QUICKLY and decisively straight up and out of THE BOX in one brisk continuous vertical pull, clearing the rim in well under a second. The BLACK top face is clearly visible as it rises. THE LEFT HAND remains planted on THE BOX.
3.0-3.6s - THE RIGHT HAND DOES NOT STOP. It keeps rising in the same continuous motion and carries THE PROJECTOR completely out of the top of frame. THE LEFT HAND stays on THE BOX at the same low height.
3.6-4.0s - THE PROJECTOR and THE RIGHT HAND are out of frame. THE LEFT HAND remains resting against the lower side of the open empty carton on the honey-oak parquet dining table with an orderly herringbone grain pattern, matte oiled timber and a clean square edge, lid flaps folded back. The printed front artwork, candle, fir, berries, fireplace and tree remain visible. Nothing else moves.

[9] CAMERA AND OPTICS - one static POV looking down at the table at roughly 50 degrees, vertical 9:16, normal wide-angle phone lens, very slight handheld drift only. The camera NEVER follows the product upward - it stays exactly where it is and lets the product exit the frame. No push-in, no orbit, no zoom, no tilt, no cut.

[10] LIGHTING - warm indoor candle and fairy-light mix, unchanged for the whole clip.

[11] PHYSICS - cardboard behaves like cardboard: the flaps crease on their folds and stay attached to THE BOX. THE BOX stays flat on the table and does not slide, tip or lift. The box rim rocks very slightly as the product is pulled out quickly, and settles against the left hand, which remains on the carton.

[12] AUDIO - DRY DOCUMENTARY AUDIO ONLY. The track contains exactly two things and nothing else: cardboard flaps creasing and fingers moving on the carton. Total silence apart from those. NO MUSIC, NO SONG, NO SCORE, NO SOUNDTRACK, NO INSTRUMENTS, NO VOICE.

[13] POSITIVE LOCKS - the lid is never lifted off, never removed, never carried aside, never set down. THE BOX never leaves the table. Exactly two hands: the right lifts the product; the left remains on the box throughout. The top face of THE PROJECTOR is BLACK. The lift out of the box is fast. THE PROJECTOR exits the top of frame and the last frame shows the open printed box on the table with the left hand still bracing it.
BANNED: any music, song, score or soundtrack; the left hand rising with the projector, leaving the box, waving, hovering or disappearing; both hands lifting the product; the right hand pausing to present the product; the product still in frame at 4.0s; changing the supplied first frame, replacing the printed box with a plain green box, erasing or redesigning the wordmark, product illustration or lettering, turning the printed front away; the camera following the product; slow motion; an all-white product top, a front lens, a swivel head, a third hand; disappearing decorations; a second box; a cut; subtitles; added overlay text.
```

### Image prompt
```text
COPY @Image 1 EXACTLY. The ONLY things that change are the hands, the countertop material and the kitchen behind them. Everything else is a literal copy.

KEEP IDENTICAL TO @Image 1: the camera position, the camera height, the lens, the distance, the vertical 9:16 crop, the exact pose of both hands, the exact grip on the product, the product's size and position and tilt in frame, where each forearm enters the frame, and the countertop.

THE COUNTERTOP MUST BE IN FRAME. Exactly as in @Image 1, the countertop occupies the LOWER RIGHT of the frame as a clean wedge running diagonally, passing behind and below the right forearm, clearly visible right up to the bottom edge of the picture. DO NOT remove the countertop. DO NOT replace the lower part of the frame with floor. NO floor is visible anywhere in this shot.

Set into the top of the dome is a flat circular BLACK perforated mesh disc. The top face is BLACK and stays black for every frame. No front lens, no camera head, no swivel.
THE LEFT HAND - a woman's hand in a soft lavender cable-knit wool sweater sleeve pushed back, no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch - resting on the left side of the dome. It does not move.
THE RIGHT HAND - a woman's hand in the same soft lavender cable-knit wool sleeve, no jewellery, entering from the right edge of frame, holding a white cable connector near the base of the stand.
THE PORT - a small dark oval USB-C socket set into the white plastic at the very bottom rim of the dome body, just above where the dome meets the white stand, a little right of the dome's centre line. It is clearly visible in the start image and it is the only opening on the product. It is NOT on the stand, NOT on the back, NOT on the top. It stays open and visible until the connector fills it, and it never disappears, closes over or moves.
THE CONNECTOR - a white plastic USB-C plug on a white cable, held between the right thumb and forefinger. Its dark flat metal tongue points LEFT, straight at THE PORT, with a gap of roughly two centimetres between the tip and the socket at 0.0s. The plug and THE PORT are already lined up on the same horizontal axis - all that is missing is the push.
COUNT LOCK: exactly two hands for the whole clip.

[7] TIMELINE
0.0-1.2s - camera completely locked. THE RIGHT HAND drives THE CONNECTOR straight to the LEFT along its own axis and PLUGS IT IN. The two-centimetre gap closes, the dark metal tongue enters THE PORT and disappears completely inside the dome, and the white plastic shoulder of the plug comes to rest flush against the white rim with no gap left between plug and dome. THIS IS THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT EVENT IN THE CLIP: the plug must visibly travel, enter the hole, and stay in it. The hand pushes once, firmly and in one go - it does not hover, hesitate, drift, retreat, tap the outside of the dome, slide along the rim, or hold the plug in mid-air. The white cable trails away to the right across the stone and stays slack. THE LEFT HAND stays still on the dome.

2.0-3.0s - the BLACK perforated mesh disc on top of the dome fills with a dense field of small RED points of light, like a red starfield, brightening from the centre outward across the black disc. A soft RED glow spills onto the stone around the base of the stand.
3.0-4.0s - the RED light settles and holds at full brightness; the small red points shimmer very slightly. THE RIGHT HAND lets go of the seated plug - which stays in THE PORT - and relaxes onto the counter beside the base. Nothing else moves.

[12] AUDIO - natural room sound only: a small plastic click as the connector seats. No music, no speech, no score.

[13] POSITIVE LOCKS - THE CONNECTOR ends the clip FULLY INSERTED into THE PORT, its metal tongue hidden inside the dome and its white shoulder flush against the white rim; anyone watching can see the plug go into the hole. The light is RED and only RED. The top face of THE PROJECTOR is BLACK and the red starfield appears ON that black disc. The countertop and the kitchen behind it are visible in every single frame. Exactly two hands. The camera never moves.
BANNED: a plug that stops short of the socket; a plug that never moves; a plug pressed against the outside of the dome or the stand instead of entering the socket; a plug pushed into the stand, the base tray, the back or the top instead of the rim socket; a plug that goes in and then comes back out; a plug left hovering in mid-air at the end; a hand that only holds or fiddles with the cable; THE PORT closing over, healing, vanishing or moving to another part of the product; a second socket appearing; blue light, white light, green light, amber light, multicoloured or rainbow light; a beam projected onto the ceiling or the walls; the product moving, lifting or rotating; the camera moving in any way; an all-white top face; the countertop or the kitchen disappearing; a third hand; a cut; subtitles; on-screen text; music.

Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. Frame 0.0s is the start image: a warm terracotta painted five-panel door with five equal horizontal recessed panels, smooth eggshell paint and fine moulding edges fully closed with a wreath, a hand on the brass knob. @Video 1 is a room-content reference for the layout, eight-facet pyramidal ceiling, furniture, the children's identities and clothes, colours, and moving projection artwork. Use the camera and child actions specified below, not the reference video's camera or its specific child actions. Only the closed-door start image defines frame 0.0s. No end frame is supplied.

THE SIX MOVING THINGS - all six running from the first frame to the last.

SMOKE. Inside the flat projected North Pole gingerbread toyshop picture, illustrated smoke curls rise from the depicted tiny chimneys, leaning right, widening and fading while fresh drawn curls form below. These curls are moving pixels lying flush on the plaster or stone. The real fireplace, mantel and chimney breast release no smoke, sparks, objects or animation into the room.

THE RIBBONS. The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons RADIATE OUTWARD FROM THE GREAT STAR and ripple as they go, like long silk curtains hung in a draught - a slow swell travels down the length of each band away from the star, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. The fan stays where it is on the plaster; the swell inside it travels.

THE SLEIGH. Santa's sleigh and its eight reindeer are FLYING ACROSS THE CEILING the way a plane crosses the sky, arcing in toward the great star on a long sweeping curve. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the ceiling and finish far across it, closer to the star than they began. Never parked.

THE NUTCRACKER PARADE AND THE ELVES. The red-and-gold nutcracker parade marches steadily along the lower wall, each soldier stepping and swinging arms at brisk real-time speed, travelling about one figure height every second. Existing elves continuously bend, lift wrapped presents and pass them toward the sleigh beside the gingerbread toyshop. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.

THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.

THE SNOW. The big soft six-point flakes and the tiny points of light fall the way heavy snow falls past a window at night - down and slightly sideways, each turning on its own centre. One fades out at the floor line as a fresh one fades in high near the ceiling, so the density never thins.

Everything else travels too: the existing tiny elves keep carrying and passing wrapped gifts beside the gingerbread toyshop, shooting stars streak and fade, the flat projected bauble and candy-cane drawings rotate and travel along the wall and ceiling surfaces, the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with their clappers swinging inside them, and the lit gingerbread toyshop windows flicker warm. Only the great gold Christmas star holds its centre position; its light simply breathes brighter and dimmer.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the ceiling is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the actual room surfaces. All eight triangular ceiling facets remain unmistakably separate real plaster planes. The red ribbons, garlands, sleigh trail and snowflakes visibly kink at every straight diagonal seam and narrow toward the central apex; every facet foreshortens the image at its own angle. Projected village buildings bend across each of the eight vertical room corners. The projecting chimney breast interrupts the village with clear bends at its two outside corners, and the window jambs and doorway lintel clip and offset the light. Raised trim throws narrow hard-edged shadows, never a seamless flat poster over the geometry. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE CEILING IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the great star, the travelling gold light waves, the rippling red-and-gold ribbons, the flying sleigh, pouring snow and twinkling stars run across the whole eight-facet pyramidal ceiling and over every ceiling seam, right into all four corners. No bare un-animated patch of plaster and no bare ceiling detail anywhere in the closing framing.

0.0-0.6s - The gentle upward tilt and gentle forward drift have ALREADY STARTED on the first frame and continue at constant speed while the hand turns the knob and pushes the door away from camera in one quick decisive shove. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.6s to the end - CONTINUE THE SAME SIMULTANEOUS FORWARD-AND-UPWARD MOVE ALREADY IN PROGRESS. Clearing the door is not a camera cue. Inside that single move, the children are playing ONE CONTINUOUS GAME OF CHASE on the open floor. They are already mid-stride before the door reveals them. The girl with deep brown skin and black hair in two puff ponytails runs a broad curving path across the rug and alongside the sofa; the boy with deep brown skin and short tightly curled hair follows two steps behind, both laughing, taking quick alternating footfalls with swinging arms. They continue their running loop naturally around the available floor space, curving away from furniture and each other. Their positions change in every successive instant, including the middle of the shot. They do not arrive at a destination, stop beside each other, face the camera, line up, switch activities or climb onto the sofa. They may glance up while their feet keep stepping, but their momentum never stops. Real speed, grounded feet and natural weight; no sliding, teleporting or running in place.

A tall rectangular smooth cream-plaster chimney breast with a walnut mantel occupies the far wall, two tall narrow black-framed windows occupy the adjoining left-hand walls, and a wide rectangular open doorway occupies the far-right wall. Warm walnut floorboards, a curved curved ivory linen sectional sofa, a round upholstered ottoman and a dark-green woven wool rug. The children are a boy of about six with deep brown skin and short tightly curled hair and a girl of about nine with deep brown skin and black hair in two puff ponytails, wearing cosy cream and burgundy knitted Christmas jumpers with green holly leaves and red-and-gold gift motifs, burgundy lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Preserve these identities and this architecture from @Video 1.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, an elaborate GINGERBREAD CHRISTMAS KINGDOM: snow-iced gingerbread houses with warm glowing windows and intricate white icing, candy-cane gateposts, a gingerbread Christmas castle covered in ruby baubles, a parade of miniature red-and-gold nutcracker toy soldiers carrying ribboned presents, wooden rocking horses and little teddy bears beside Christmas trees. A large decorated fir tree rises from the village square with gold candle lights, scarlet bows and perfectly drawn ornaments. Santa's elves in red-and-green winter clothes load presents onto a red sleigh outside a gingerbread toyshop. Along the upper walls, enormous evergreen wreaths, paired brass Christmas bells with velvet bows, holly leaves and illuminated stockings. ACROSS THE WHOLE CEILING, Santa in his red suit and eight reindeer circle a luminous gold eight-point Christmas star; scalloped red ribbons, evergreen garlands, gold baubles and fine cream snowflakes radiate toward the surrounding walls. Crimson red, warm gold and evergreen green dominate; gingerbread stays warmly illuminated, never muddy. Dense coherent storybook Christmas detail on the wall and ceiling surfaces only. Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. Frame 0.0s is the start image: a warm terracotta painted five-panel door with five equal horizontal recessed panels, smooth eggshell paint and fine moulding edges fully closed with a wreath, a hand on the brass knob. @Video 1 is a room-content reference for the layout, eight-facet pyramidal ceiling, furniture, the children's identities and clothes, colours, and moving projection artwork. Use the camera and child actions specified below, not the reference video's camera or its specific child actions. Only the closed-door start image defines frame 0.0s. No end frame is supplied.

THE SIX MOVING THINGS - all six running from the first frame to the last.

SMOKE. Inside the flat projected North Pole gingerbread toyshop picture, illustrated smoke curls rise from the depicted tiny chimneys, leaning right, widening and fading while fresh drawn curls form below. These curls are moving pixels lying flush on the plaster or stone. The real fireplace, mantel and chimney breast release no smoke, sparks, objects or animation into the room.

THE RIBBONS. The crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbons RADIATE OUTWARD FROM THE GREAT STAR and ripple as they go, like long silk curtains hung in a draught - a slow swell travels down the length of each band away from the star, brightening it as it passes and combing its lower fringe into new folds. The fan stays where it is on the plaster; the swell inside it travels.

THE SLEIGH. Santa's sleigh and its eight reindeer are FLYING ACROSS THE CEILING the way a plane crosses the sky, arcing in toward the great star on a long sweeping curve. Legs gallop, heads nod, the sleigh rocks, the long trail of light streams off the runners, the team rises and dips. They start near one side of the ceiling and finish far across it, closer to the star than they began. Never parked.

THE NUTCRACKER PARADE AND THE ELVES. The red-and-gold nutcracker parade marches steadily along the lower wall, each soldier stepping and swinging arms at brisk real-time speed, travelling about one figure height every second. Existing elves continuously bend, lift wrapped presents and pass them toward the sleigh beside the gingerbread toyshop. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.

THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.

THE SNOW. The big soft six-point flakes and the tiny points of light fall the way heavy snow falls past a window at night - down and slightly sideways, each turning on its own centre. One fades out at the floor line as a fresh one fades in high near the ceiling, so the density never thins.

Everything else travels too: the existing tiny elves keep carrying and passing wrapped gifts beside the gingerbread toyshop, shooting stars streak and fade, the flat projected bauble and candy-cane drawings rotate and travel along the wall and ceiling surfaces, the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with their clappers swinging inside them, and the lit gingerbread toyshop windows flicker warm. Only the great gold Christmas star holds its centre position; its light simply breathes brighter and dimmer.

ONE PROJECTOR, ONE PICTURE: whatever the wall is doing, the ceiling is doing at the same instant and the same speed. The picture's outer boundary is anchored - it never rotates, spins or slides across the room as one sheet; only the things inside it travel. It is flat light lying on the actual room surfaces. All eight triangular ceiling facets remain unmistakably separate real plaster planes. The red ribbons, garlands, sleigh trail and snowflakes visibly kink at every straight diagonal seam and narrow toward the central apex; every facet foreshortens the image at its own angle. Projected village buildings bend across each of the eight vertical room corners. The projecting chimney breast interrupts the village with clear bends at its two outside corners, and the window jambs and doorway lintel clip and offset the light. Raised trim throws narrow hard-edged shadows, never a seamless flat poster over the geometry. Nothing ever leaves a surface, and nothing is projected on the floor, rug, sofa, table, tree or gifts.

THE CEILING IS FULL. The ceiling carries as much moving picture as the walls do, everywhere it appears in frame and above all in the closing seconds: the great star, the travelling gold light waves, the rippling red-and-gold ribbons, the flying sleigh, pouring snow and twinkling stars run across the whole eight-facet pyramidal ceiling and over every ceiling seam, right into all four corners. No bare un-animated patch of plaster and no bare ceiling detail anywhere in the closing framing.

BEATS
0.0-0.6s - The gentle upward tilt and gentle forward drift have ALREADY STARTED on the first frame and continue at constant speed while the hand turns the knob and pushes the door away from camera in one quick decisive shove. The room behind it is already running AND BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY IN MOTION - the widening gap only uncovers more of a movie that was already playing and two kids who were already playing actively before the door opened. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap.
0.6s to the end - CONTINUE THE SAME SIMULTANEOUS FORWARD-AND-UPWARD MOVE ALREADY IN PROGRESS. Clearing the door is not a camera cue. The view must not travel straight ahead first and only later rise to the ceiling. The camera advances slowly through the doorway while its aim keeps rising at the same small constant angular rate that began at 0.0s; room features slide downward through the frame continuously as perspective gently advances. No hold on the children, no stop at the sofa, no separate ceiling reveal. Do not speed up the tilt in the last two seconds to finish a composition. The shot simply ends during the same ongoing gentle movement, with both back wall and ceiling still visible. All through it the smoke pours from every chimney, the ribbons ripples, the sleigh flies on across the ceiling, the nutcracker parade and the elves keep moving, the travelling gold light waves keep spreading and the snow keeps falling.
Inside that single move, 0.0-1.5s: the boy is already taking quick grounded steps toward the open side of the sofa, and the girl is already stepping across the rug, looking up with animated delight and lifting one arm to track the flying sleigh. 1.5-2.8s: the boy makes one short natural hop onto the broad interior seat cushion, landing with both feet near its centre and bending his knees; the cushion visibly compresses. The girl takes two more small steps while sweeping her pointing hand along the moving ceiling picture, then lowers her arm and turns her shoulders toward him, laughing. 2.8-5.0s: the boy immediately continues two small balanced bounces on the same cushion centre. The girl keeps stepping and shifting her weight, looks back up and points again with a changing arm angle as the sleigh travels. No hold between these actions, no arrival pause, no paired pose, no matching a reference-video frame. Both have normal anatomy, grounded foot contact and natural real-time weight. These child beats do not introduce any new camera beat; the same constant simultaneous forward-and-upward camera move continues unchanged.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, subtle natural handheld sway. One blended forward-and-upward move, with constant gentle upward angular speed across all five seconds. No delayed tilt, no pitch jump, no sudden reframe, no acceleration or speed ramp, no stop, no second move, no cut, no zoom, no orbit, no fisheye, no lens warping. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame after 0.6s.

EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN, no adults, no third child, no pets. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - the panel edges of the door, the brass knob, the forearm and sleeve exactly as shown in the starting image, with no jewellery. Crisp phone footage, in focus first frame to last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, quick footfalls, soft sofa-cushion landings, children laughing, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS - real-time motion at normal speed. One continuous take. One single smooth camera move with forward translation and upward tilt happening simultaneously from the first frame, at unchanged gentle speed throughout. The projected picture is visibly in motion in every single frame, at the start, in the middle and in the last second. The whole eight-facet pyramidal ceiling is covered in moving picture at the end. BOTH CHILDREN ARE MOVING IN EVERY FRAME THEY APPEAR IN, from the first frame the door uncovers them to the last frame of the clip: the boy steps, hops onto the broad sofa seat and keeps bouncing while the girl keeps stepping, looking, pointing, lowering her arm and turning; the camera may tilt them out of view but never catches them settled into a pose. Their movement is natural, unchoreographed, real-speed kid movement. Sharp focus to the final frame.

BANNED - a still or frozen picture; a projection that appears to move only because the camera moves; light that moves for a moment then stops; chimneys without smoke; an ribbons that sits still; a parked sleigh; a nutcracker parade that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that twinkle but never travel; a still ceiling above a moving wall; an empty or un-animated area of ceiling or an un-animated patch of the vault in any frame; the whole picture rotating or sliding as one sheet; a pattern that looks printed, painted or like wallpaper; a glow or bright seam along the door edge or gap; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; particles falling through the open air of the room; volumetric snow; snow in front of the children, the sofa, the stair or the real tree; a camera that moves, stops and then moves again; two separate camera moves; a forward-only push followed by a late upward tilt; holding a level angle until entering the room; a sudden ceiling swing; accelerating the tilt near the end; copying @Video 1's camera timing; a child balancing or landing on the sofa backrest or outer edge; either child standing still, waiting, hesitating, posed or rooted to one spot at any point; a child who only turns her head while her feet stay planted; a child who begins the clip motionless and starts moving later; a child who stops moving before the last frame; the projector unit visible; slow motion; blur; any music; a cut; an adult; subtitles; on-screen text.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the nutcracker parade advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

REFERENCE-ONLY LOCK - @Video 1 provides room, identity and projection appearance only. No static room image is attached. Preserve the closed-door starting frame and the established single walking-and-tilting camera move. The children perform the continuous sofa-hop and moving-pointing actions specified above; their actions are independent of all poses and activity changes in the reference video. Never copy the reference video's opening composition, sofa bouncing, camera movement, cuts or timing. The new clip is exactly 5 seconds and has no end frame.

SURFACE-ONLY LOCK - every animated Christmas shape, including every shape above the real fireplace, is flat projected light attached to the existing plaster, chimney surface and ceiling. Moving drawings follow the surface perspective and bend or clip at actual corners and ceiling details. They have no thickness, volume, free-space parallax or physical cast shadow. The real fireplace opening and mantel remain real stationary architecture. No character, sleigh, ribbon, spark, snowflake, smoke curl or ornament floats between the camera and a wall, emerges from the fireplace, hovers above the mantel, or enters the usable room. Apparent depth belongs only to the drawn illustration on the wall.

ROOM AND IDENTITY - a grand OCTAGONAL Christmas sitting room with eight straight cream-plaster walls and an eight-sided PYRAMIDAL CEILING: eight broad triangular plaster facets rising from the eight horizontal wall tops to one single central apex, each adjacent facet meeting on a clean straight diagonal seam, no curved vault, no flat coffer ceiling and no trusses. A tall rectangular smooth cream-plaster chimney breast with a walnut mantel occupies the far wall, two tall narrow black-framed windows occupy the adjoining left-hand walls, and a wide rectangular open doorway occupies the far-right wall. Warm walnut floorboards, a curved curved ivory linen sectional sofa, a round upholstered ottoman and a dark-green woven wool rug. The children are a boy of about six with deep brown skin and short tightly curled hair and a girl of about nine with deep brown skin and black hair in two puff ponytails, wearing cosy cream and burgundy knitted Christmas jumpers with green holly leaves and red-and-gold gift motifs, burgundy lounge trousers and cream wool socks. Preserve these identities and this architecture from @Video 1.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, an elaborate GINGERBREAD CHRISTMAS KINGDOM: snow-iced gingerbread houses with warm glowing windows and intricate white icing, candy-cane gateposts, a gingerbread Christmas castle covered in ruby baubles, a parade of miniature red-and-gold nutcracker toy soldiers carrying ribboned presents, wooden rocking horses and little teddy bears beside Christmas trees. A large decorated fir tree rises from the village square with gold candle lights, scarlet bows and perfectly drawn ornaments. Santa's elves in red-and-green winter clothes load presents onto a red sleigh outside a gingerbread toyshop. Along the upper walls, enormous evergreen wreaths, paired brass Christmas bells with velvet bows, holly leaves and illuminated stockings. ACROSS THE WHOLE CEILING, Santa in his red suit and eight reindeer circle a luminous gold eight-point Christmas star; scalloped red ribbons, evergreen garlands, gold baubles and fine cream snowflakes radiate toward the surrounding walls. Crimson red, warm gold and evergreen green dominate; gingerbread stays warmly illuminated, never muddy. Dense coherent storybook Christmas detail on the wall and ceiling surfaces only. No carnival rides, no sci-fi nebula, no space vortex, no physical floating objects in the room.

A tall rectangular smooth cream-plaster chimney breast with a walnut mantel occupies the far wall, two tall narrow black-framed windows occupy the adjoining left-hand walls, and a wide rectangular open doorway occupies the far-right wall. Seen from the doorway; the new a warm terracotta painted five-panel door with five equal horizontal recessed panels, smooth eggshell paint and fine moulding edges is open out of frame.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red baubles, gold ribbon and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-cream knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, an elaborate GINGERBREAD CHRISTMAS KINGDOM: snow-iced gingerbread houses with warm glowing windows and intricate white icing, candy-cane gateposts, a gingerbread Christmas castle covered in ruby baubles, a parade of miniature red-and-gold nutcracker toy soldiers carrying ribboned presents, wooden rocking horses and little teddy bears beside Christmas trees. A large decorated fir tree rises from the village square with gold candle lights, scarlet bows and perfectly drawn ornaments. Santa's elves in red-and-green winter clothes load presents onto a red sleigh outside a gingerbread toyshop. Along the upper walls, enormous evergreen wreaths, paired brass Christmas bells with velvet bows, holly leaves and illuminated stockings. ACROSS THE WHOLE CEILING, Santa in his red suit and eight reindeer circle a luminous gold eight-point Christmas star; scalloped red ribbons, evergreen garlands, gold baubles and fine cream snowflakes radiate toward the surrounding walls. Crimson red, warm gold and evergreen green dominate; gingerbread stays warmly illuminated, never muddy. Dense coherent storybook Christmas detail on the wall and ceiling surfaces only. No carnival rides, no sci-fi nebula, no space vortex, no physical floating objects in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   All eight triangular ceiling facets remain unmistakably separate real plaster planes. The red ribbons, garlands, sleigh trail and snowflakes visibly kink at every straight diagonal seam and narrow toward the central apex; every facet foreshortens the image at its own angle. Projected village buildings bend across each of the eight vertical room corners. The projecting chimney breast interrupts the village with clear bends at its two outside corners, and the window jambs and doorway lintel clip and offset the light. Raised trim throws narrow hard-edged shadows, never a seamless flat poster over the geometry. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the knit stitches of the jumpers, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the surface texture of the cream plaster and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Wholesome family Christmas scene, a child at home with his family at Christmas.

SUMMARY: a boy bounces on his bed in a Christmas bedroom at night while a projected Christmas village picture is PLAYING across the cream plaster wall behind the bed and the whole mansard ceiling above it, thrown there by a projector standing on the floor. 5 seconds, one continuous handheld phone take, vertical.

GLOBAL STYLE: crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed. Sharp and in focus from the first frame to the last, at least as sharp as the start image. No blur, no haze, no soft focus, no washed-out exposure, no colour shift.

ASSET ROLES: @Image 1 is frame 0.0s and controls a bedroom under a symmetrical MANSARD CEILING: one wide flat cream-plaster central strip with a broad straight slope descending from each of its two long edges to the left and right walls; two clean parallel ceiling seams and no beams or tray recesses. A dark walnut bed with a rounded upholstered oatmeal headboard is centred on the back wall, a small dormer window recess opens from the left slope and red velvet curtains frame its glass. Walnut nightstands, warm walnut floorboards and a deep-green woven wool rug. Preserve the exact furniture placement, child identities, clothing, room colours and framing in that supplied image.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about six with deep brown skin and short tightly curled hair, wearing cosy cream and burgundy knitted Christmas jumpers with green holly leaves and red-and-gold gift motifs, burgundy lounge trousers and cream wool socks, standing on the bed with his arms thrown wide. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the cream plaster wall and the whole mansard ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, an elaborate GINGERBREAD CHRISTMAS KINGDOM: snow-iced gingerbread houses with warm glowing windows and intricate white icing, candy-cane gateposts, a gingerbread Christmas castle covered in ruby baubles, a parade of miniature red-and-gold nutcracker toy soldiers carrying ribboned presents, wooden rocking horses and little teddy bears beside Christmas trees. A large decorated fir tree rises from the village square with gold candle lights, scarlet bows and perfectly drawn ornaments. Santa's elves in red-and-green winter clothes load presents onto a red sleigh outside a gingerbread toyshop. Along the upper walls, enormous evergreen wreaths, paired brass Christmas bells with velvet bows, holly leaves and illuminated stockings. ACROSS THE WHOLE CEILING, Santa in his red suit and eight reindeer circle a luminous gold eight-point Christmas star; scalloped red ribbons, evergreen garlands, gold baubles and fine cream snowflakes radiate toward the surrounding walls. Crimson red, warm gold and evergreen green dominate; gingerbread stays warmly illuminated, never muddy. Dense coherent storybook Christmas detail on the wall and ceiling surfaces only. No carnival rides, no sci-fi nebula, no space vortex, no physical floating objects in the room.

HOW THE PICTURE BEHAVES - THIS IS THE ENTIRE POINT OF THE CLIP. In every previous attempt the picture sat dead still like printed wallpaper and the clip was worthless. THE PICTURE IS A FILM PLAYING ON A CINEMA SCREEN: the screen is the plaster and holds perfectly still, while everything inside the picture is in constant motion for all five seconds. The supplied starting frame already has the complete projection at full brightness. Continue motion inside that existing picture immediately; no fade-in or gradual appearance. Matching @Image 1 applies to the bedroom, the bed, THE BOY and the colours, not to where any element of the picture sits.
THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
- THE CHIMNEY SMOKE - every single chimney in the village is smoking, not one of them without its plume. Each thin curl climbs upward out of its chimney pot, leaning slightly to the right, rising about one roof height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the ribbons, while a fresh curl forms at the pot behind it. The plumes are never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the village chimneys and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the chimneys continuously so the column never empties.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE NUTCRACKER PARADE AND THE ELVES. The red-and-gold nutcracker parade marches steadily along the lower wall, each soldier stepping and swinging arms at brisk real-time speed, travelling about one figure height every second. Existing elves continuously bend, lift wrapped presents and pass them toward the sleigh beside the gingerbread toyshop. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE ELVES AND THE FLOATING THINGS - the tiny elves keep passing gifts outside the gingerbread toyshop, and the floating baubles and candy canes drift through the scene at their own depths, each one turning slowly as it goes, while the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with the clappers moving inside them.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the skirting board fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the seams of the ceiling, so the density holds even. Track this one so the fall is unmistakable: a large cream six-point flake that begins just above the headboard, falls steadily and drifts slightly right, passes the middle of the wall at 2.5s and fades out low near the skirting at 5.0s.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster.

TIMELINE - THE CAMERA MAKES EXACTLY TWO MOVES AND NO MORE, and they are the opposite of the other clips in this set: this one starts high and comes DOWN.
0.0-2.2s the camera is aimed HIGH at the start, weighted to the top of the cream plaster wall and the mansard ceiling so the projected picture fills most of the frame and THE BOY is small and low near the bottom edge. From 0.0s the camera TILTS DOWN in one slow steady continuous move, the ribbons rippling, the travelling gold light waves spreading and the sleigh flying across the mansard ceiling as it goes, until the bed and THE BOY fill the lower half. THE BOY bends his knees and pushes off the mattress; both feet leave the quilt, arms swinging up over his head, mouth open laughing. The mattress compresses and springs back.
2.2-5.0s the tilt ends and the camera PULLS BACK a little in one slow steady move, the frame opening slightly wider so a little more of the bedroom comes in at the edges - a small gentle zoom out, nothing sudden. THE BOY lands on both feet and drives straight into a second, higher jump, knees tucking, then a third; he never stops moving and the body motion carries straight across the seam between the two camera moves.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed - a real boy jumping at real speed and landing with real weight. Exactly one child. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes exactly two moves: a slow tilt DOWN, then a small gentle pull back. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Wall and ceiling always move together. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a camera that tilts UP; a camera that pushes in; more than two camera moves; a hard or fast zoom; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; an ribbons that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a nutcracker parade that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above a moving wall; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the nutcracker parade advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The flat central ceiling strip and two broad slopes remain clearly legible. The sleigh, red ribbons and garlands break perspective at the two parallel seams and stretch down each sloped side. At the left dormer the picture steps into the recess, changing angle on its cheeks and lintel, with narrow cast shadows. Where the slopes meet the vertical walls, the Christmas picture bends again. No flat image suspended in front of the architecture. All physical walls, ceiling planes, beams, seams and trim remain stationary.
```

A tall rectangular smooth cream-plaster chimney breast with a walnut mantel occupies the far wall, two tall narrow black-framed windows occupy the adjoining left-hand walls, and a wide rectangular open doorway occupies the far-right wall. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-cream knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 3 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, an elaborate GINGERBREAD CHRISTMAS KINGDOM: snow-iced gingerbread houses with warm glowing windows and intricate white icing, candy-cane gateposts, a gingerbread Christmas castle covered in ruby baubles, a parade of miniature red-and-gold nutcracker toy soldiers carrying ribboned presents, wooden rocking horses and little teddy bears beside Christmas trees. A large decorated fir tree rises from the village square with gold candle lights, scarlet bows and perfectly drawn ornaments. Santa's elves in red-and-green winter clothes load presents onto a red sleigh outside a gingerbread toyshop. Along the upper walls, enormous evergreen wreaths, paired brass Christmas bells with velvet bows, holly leaves and illuminated stockings. ACROSS THE WHOLE CEILING, Santa in his red suit and eight reindeer circle a luminous gold eight-point Christmas star; scalloped red ribbons, evergreen garlands, gold baubles and fine cream snowflakes radiate toward the surrounding walls. Crimson red, warm gold and evergreen green dominate; gingerbread stays warmly illuminated, never muddy. Dense coherent storybook Christmas detail on the wall and ceiling surfaces only. No carnival rides, no sci-fi nebula, no space vortex, no physical floating objects in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   All eight triangular ceiling facets remain unmistakably separate real plaster planes. The red ribbons, garlands, sleigh trail and snowflakes visibly kink at every straight diagonal seam and narrow toward the central apex; every facet foreshortens the image at its own angle. Projected village buildings bend across each of the eight vertical room corners. The projecting chimney breast interrupts the village with clear bends at its two outside corners, and the window jambs and doorway lintel clip and offset the light. Raised trim throws narrow hard-edged shadows, never a seamless flat poster over the geometry. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The timber floorboards, the dark-green woven wool rug, the curved ivory linen sectional sofa, the long upholstered ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children catch that spill so their knitted jumpers read clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no round side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain rug and floor.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the knit stitches of the jumpers, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the surface texture of the cream plaster and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.

- THE CHIMNEY SMOKE - every single chimney in the village is smoking, not one of them without its plume. Each thin curl climbs upward out of its chimney pot, leaning slightly to the right, rising about one roof height every second, widening and thinning as it climbs and fading out before it reaches the ribbons, while a fresh curl forms at the pot behind it. The plumes are never a fixed painted shape.
- THE RISING SPARKS - a slow steady rise of glowing gold sparks lifts off the village chimneys and climbs the full height of the wall to join the star field, drifting slightly sideways as it goes, new sparks leaving the chimneys continuously so the column never empties.
- THE RIBBONS - each crimson, ruby, gold and emerald ribbon radiates outward from the great star and ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the star, about a third of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the wall and on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the great star, covering about a fifth of the ceiling's width every second, so by 4.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes. The team is never parked.
- THE NUTCRACKER PARADE AND THE ELVES. The red-and-gold nutcracker parade marches steadily along the lower wall, each soldier stepping and swinging arms at brisk real-time speed, travelling about one figure height every second. Existing elves continuously bend, lift wrapped presents and pass them toward the sleigh beside the gingerbread toyshop. All keep moving from the first frame to the last.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about a third of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - each one drifts downward and slightly sideways about a quarter of the wall's height every second, the way snow falls past a window at night. Each turns slowly on its own centre as it drifts and breathes brighter and dimmer on its own timing. One that reaches the floor line fades out there while a fresh one fades in high near the plaster ceiling details, so the density holds even.
- THE SMALL TRAVELLERS - the red-and-white striped candy canes and the glowing baubles turn slowly end over end as they drift; the tiny elves keep passing gifts beside the gingerbread toyshop and the big gold bells swing gently on their crimson bow with the clappers moving inside them; the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade, with fresh ones setting off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE GREAT CHRISTMAS STAR - the gold eight-pointed star is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the back wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster, wall material and ceiling details. NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR: the pale oak floorboards, the dark-green woven wool rug, the curved ivory linen sectional sofa, the long ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry no projected shapes at all.

TIMELINE:
0.0-1.0s the person filming takes one clear step forward; the frame moves in and settles. THE OTHER CHILD bounces on the sofa cushions with both arms up, laughing. THE SMALLER CHILD turns on the rug with his face tipped up.
1.0-2.0s the camera holds in the closer position. THE OTHER CHILD pushes up harder and drops back down onto the cushion, her knees bending and springing back. THE SMALLER CHILD keeps turning and tips his head further back.
2.0-4.0s the camera tilts up in one slow smooth move past the top of the tree until the eight-facet pyramidal ceiling fills the upper half of the frame, the ribbons rippling along it, the travelling gold light waves still spreading outward across it from the great star and the sleigh team still flying in toward that star. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing and squealing, feet thumping on the sofa cushions, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed throughout - real children moving at real speed with real weight. Exactly two children, and their knitted jumpers read clearly. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes one move per beat and no more. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Walls and ceiling always move together. THE OTHER CHILD stays on the sofa. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: blank or partly blank walls or ceiling at the start; a projection appearing, switching on, fading in, spreading, growing or revealing itself during the shot; an opening before-and-after transformation; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; an ribbons that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; a nutcracker parade that does not travel or elves that do not move; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above moving walls; an un-animated area of the eight-facet pyramidal ceiling or an un-animated ceiling detail in any frame; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; children standing still; a third child; an adult; the projector unit in shot; jellyfish, fish, coral or bubbles; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the nutcracker parade advances along the wall; elves move and pass gifts; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - All eight triangular ceiling facets remain unmistakably separate real plaster planes. The red ribbons, garlands, sleigh trail and snowflakes visibly kink at every straight diagonal seam and narrow toward the central apex; every facet foreshortens the image at its own angle. Projected village buildings bend across each of the eight vertical room corners. The projecting chimney breast interrupts the village with clear bends at its two outside corners, and the window jambs and doorway lintel clip and offset the light. Raised trim throws narrow hard-edged shadows, never a seamless flat poster over the geometry. All physical walls, ceiling planes, beams, seams and trim remain stationary.
```

### Image prompt
```text
REFERENCE ROLES: the FIRST attached image owns the geometry, camera position, framing, pose and prop placement. The SECOND attached image is only a photographic-look reference for clear natural iPhone detail, realistic materials and colour; do not copy its people, room or composition. The new people and room are specified below. Preserve the FIRST image's composition.

Extremely high quality candid vertical iPhone photo taken from the floor of a double-height Christmas living room, looking up and across at a woman standing on the mezzanine landing, evening. We see her from below and slightly to the side, her raised arms and the high ceiling in front of her.

FRAMING AND ANGLE: the camera stands on the living-room floor MUCH closer in to the stairs than a wide establishing shot, tilted up, so the woman and the inflatable she is taping fill most of the picture and the room reads as context around them rather than as the subject. The mezzanine landing crosses the frame with its black metal railing along it, and the woman stands on that landing behind the railing DEAD CENTRE on the vertical axis of the frame, framed from roughly the waist up and very large in the picture - she is the biggest thing in the photograph and the taped inflatable is directly above and ahead of her, also near the centre, so her reaching arm and the inflatable together sit in the middle of the frame. A short stretch of the open white staircase still enters the lower right corner, and the living-room floor is still clearly visible below her in the lower part of the picture, so the drop from the landing to the floor reads plainly. She is NOT on a ladder - there is no ladder anywhere in the picture.

THE KEY ELEMENT: a large inflatable Santa Claus decoration is TAPED FLAT TO THE HIGH WHITE CEILING above the double-height space, lying on its back against the plaster and facing down into the room, held by a mess of criss-crossed silver duct tape strips over its legs, arms and hat. It is clearly fixed to the ceiling. It hangs out over the open double-height void rather than over the landing. One corner has started to peel, with a long strip of tape hanging down.

HER POSE - THIS IS THE POINT OF THE PICTURE: she is leaning far out over the landing railing to reach it. Her hips are pressed against the top rail, her weight is tipped forward past it and out over the open void, her feet still on the landing behind her with her heels lifting, one hand gripping the rail and the other stretched up and out pressing a fresh strip of silver duct tape onto Santa's shoulder, a roll of duct tape in her gripping hand. She is visibly overreaching and off balance, the rail the only thing holding her, empty air and the floor below her.

THE ROOM: a double-height American transitional living room - pale dove-grey painted walls with white picture-frame moulding, a wall of tall black-framed windows at the right with a dark winter garden behind them, a white open-tread staircase with slim black metal spindles, a mezzanine landing with a matching black rail and a green garland with red bows wound along it, a pale limestone fireplace on the ground floor, a tall green Christmas tree with warm white lights and red and gold baubles standing in the double-height space, honey-oak wide-plank floor, a camel linen sofa and a muted blue and cream rug.

A UNITED STATES FLAG: a real fabric United States flag hangs flat against the pale dove-grey wall in the background under the landing, clearly readable stars and stripes, hanging straight and still, about the size of a large poster. It does not cover the Christmas tree or the fireplace and it does not reach up into the empty band of ceiling at the top of the frame.

HER CLOTHES: matching American-flag Christmas pyjamas - a pyjama top with bold red and white horizontal stripes and a navy blue yoke across the shoulders covered in small white stars, and matching red and white striped pyjama bottoms, soft brushed cotton, slightly loose the way real pyjamas fit - with thick grey wool socks. She is early 30s with copper-red hair in a loose low bun, fair freckled skin and a softly rounded face.

There is no projected light anywhere - the ceiling, the walls and the staircase are plain painted plaster and plain timber, completely clean, no projected patterns, no snowflakes, no stars, no coloured light spilling on any surface. The room is lit only by the Christmas tree, the fireplace and ordinary warm lamps.

COLOUR - READ THIS CAREFULLY: neutral white balance, the way an iPhone auto-corrects a warm room. The picture is NOT orange, NOT amber, NOT sepia, NOT golden-hour, and there is no warm colour cast laid over the whole frame. The ceiling reads as clean white paint and the walls as pale dove-grey paint, the window frames and the rail read as true black, the oak floor reads as natural wood, the flag keeps true red, white and navy. The Christmas tree lights, the fireplace and the lamps are warm points of light sitting inside a neutrally balanced room - they do not tint the rest of the picture.

LIGHTING AND QUALITY: bright, clean and evenly lit - plenty of light on the high ceiling, on the landing and on her, no dark corners, no heavy shadows. Shot on an iPhone and straight out of the camera roll: an ordinary everyday snapshot, not a magazine photo and not a rendered image. Modern phone-camera look - wide dynamic range, lifted shadows, clean highlights, accurate colour. Extremely high quality, tack sharp and in focus everywhere, crisp detail in the duct tape, the inflatable vinyl sheen and the weave of her pyjamas, realistic skin and fabric texture. Absolutely nothing blurry: no motion blur, no soft focus, no background blur, no grain, no film look, no cinematic grade, no vignette, no bloom. Normal wide-angle lens, vertical lines vertical and parallel, no fisheye distortion.

FRAMING: leave a generous clean band of plain empty ceiling across the very top of the image - roughly the top fifth of the frame - completely empty and free of Santa, of tape, of beams, of garland and of any other object, so a caption can sit there. Make that room by tilting the camera up slightly, NOT by pulling the camera back and NOT by shrinking the woman - she stays close and large and centred exactly as described above.

No captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
```
